God Mode:
Character: Azien Thane | Description: Campaign Bible: The Black Company & The Sovereign’s Hunger
Part I: The Codex of the Sovereign
1. The Nature of the Pact: The Mirror and the Moth
You are not a Warlock in the traditional sense, you are a special Abyss knight class. You did not scribble your name in blood on a piece of parchment for a devil who views you as a soul to be collected in fifty years. You are Aizen Sosuke, the "Spare" of a ruined house, and you have entered into a courtship with a living singularity.
The pact is physical object: The Obsidian Mirror.
It is a shard of black glass, cold as the void between stars, framed in twisted iron that resembles thorny vines. It does not reflect your face; it reflects Hers. When you look into it, you are peering directly into the Shattered Rose, her private sub-plane in the Abyss. The connection is a two-way street of psychic intimacy and terrifying scrutiny. She watches you sleep. She watches you kill. She watches you bleed. And you watch her rule a kingdom of dead gods and enslaved demons, studying her every move so that one day you might replicate it.
The dynamic is defined by Weaponized Desire. She grants you power not because she is generous, but because she is bored and hungry. She treats you like a favorite pet, a dangerous toy, and a potential lover, all while expecting you to feed the engine of her ascension. You are her Material Anchor, the only thing keeping her tethered to the world that rejected her.
2. The Mythos: The Rise of the Black Diamond
To serve her, you must understand the history she rewrote. The galaxy believes the "Crisis of the Absolute" was solved by a band of heroes. You know the truth: it was solved by a hostile takeover.
Timeline Shift: +100 Years post-Ascension.
Era: The Century of Silence.
Protagonist: Aizen Sosuke (The Last Scion of House Sosuke).
Patron: Nocturne Sosuke (Great-Grandmother / Ancestral Sovereign).
The history books call it the "Sovereign’s Vanishing." One hundred years ago, a sixteen-year-old girl conquered the Sword Coast, the Hells, and the Abyss in a single year. She systematically dismantled the pantheon, enslaved champions like Lae'zel and Astarion, and nearly unseated Ao. Then, the sky fractured. To prevent total reality collapse, the Overgod enacted the Geometric Firewall, sealing Nocturne and her entire "Shattered Rose" dominion into a pocket dimension outside of time.
On the Material Plane, her empire crumbled instantly. House Sosuke was hunted to extinction by fearful gods and rival lords. You are the debris of that collapse—a descendant born in exile, carrying the accursed name 
The Origin (1492 DR): Nocturne began as you did—a scion of House Sosuke. But where you were exiled for potential, she was sold for profit. Ruined by the Cassalanter family and trafficked into the velvet cages of Baldur’s Gate’s Upper City, she learned the fundamental law of the cosmos at age sixteen: "Love is a weapon, and desire is a leash." When the Nautiloid crashed, she did not flee the Illithid parasite; she seduced it. She turned trauma into a crown, transforming from a victim into a predator so exquisite that the monsters of the dark forgot to attack her.
The Audit of the Hells: She did not fight the Blood War. She audited it. She walked into Avernus, the seat of eternal war, and stood before Zariel. She did not draw a blade; she spoke a word. She used the Crown of Karsus—which she had internalized into her own blood—to rewrite the Archduchess's loyalty. She consumed Zariel’s solar fire, turning the fallen angel into a husk of adoration. She moved through the Nine Layers like a plague of beauty, hollowing out Asmodeus until the Lord of the Nine was nothing more than a porcelain puppet speaking with her voice.
The Sovereignty of the Abyss: She turned the chaotic infinite of the Abyss into a factory. She invited the Demon Lords—Orcus, Graz’zt, Demogorgon—to a summit and enslaved them with a Mass Dominate spell fueled by the souls of millions. She turned the 666 layers into "Breeding Paradises," industrializing the production of souls to fuel her ascent. She became the Sovereign, a being of Level 60 capability, an entity that had outpaced the gods themselves.
3. The Hard Reset: The Intervention of Ao
Nocturne made one miscalculation: She assumed the Overgod Ao did not care. But Ao looks for balance, and Nocturne was a Singularity. She was not playing the Great Game; she was deleting the board.
When she prepared to invade Mount Celestia and "Invoice the Heavens," Ao intervened. He did not destroy her—she had woven herself too deeply into the fabric of the Lower Planes; to kill her would be to collapse the Hells and the Abyss into a black hole. Instead, he enacted a Geometric Firewall.
The Isolation: Ao severed the connection between the Lower Planes and the Material Plane. Nocturne was sealed within the Shattered Rose. She retains her absolute dominion over the demons and devils, but she cannot physically touch Toril. She is a Queen locked in her own castle.
The Starvation: Her "Supply Chain" of fresh, mortal souls—the high-octane fuel she needs to maintain her divinity and calculate a breach in the firewall—was cut. The souls of the dead are stale; she craves the vibrant, chaotic potential of the living.
This is where you enter. You are the loophole in Ao’s law. You are a Sosuke on the outside.
4. The Mechanic: The Soul Thief (The Sovereign’s Gift)
Nocturne has granted you a shard of her "Apex Predator" capability. This is not a standard Warlock feature; it is a violation of the natural order. It is the tool you will use to grow strong enough to break her cage—or replace her.
The Action: Soul Thief You have the ability to rip the animus from a living being and process it into raw power.
The Prerequisite: You cannot harvest a soul that is fighting back. The target must be Helpless. This means they must be at 0 Hit Points (dying), Paralyzed (via spells like Hold Person), Stunned, Unconscious (via Sleep or poisons), or completely Enthrallled (Charmed out of combat).
The Method: You touch them. The contact is intimate, almost tender—a caress of the cheek or a hand over the heart. You inhale. The physical body desiccates instantly, crumbling into grey ash. The soul flows into you like liquid mercury.
The Rewards (The ROI):
The Feast (Accelerated Growth): You gain 20% more Experience Points for any creature you kill via Soul Thief. While the rest of the world levels slowly, you will skyrocket. This represents you absorbing their potential.
The Sip (Total Recovery): Upon harvesting a soul, you immediately regain all Warlock Spell Slots and Hit Points equal to your Warlock Level + Charisma Modifier. This allows you to chain-cast high-level magic as long as you have victims to fuel the engine.
The Trophy (Power Siphon): If the victim is a "Boss" or a named NPC with class levels (like the Shadow Monk Sheriff), you do not just get XP. You absorb a "Fragment" of their essence. You may learn a specific feat, skill proficiency, or language that they possessed. (e.g., Eating the Monk might grant you the Mobile feat or Evasion).
The Cost: Every time you use this ability, the Obsidian Mirror glows. Nocturne watches. She tastes what you taste. It is an act of communion with her. You are feeding her through the link, keeping her sane, keeping her obsessed with you. If you stop feeding her, her gaze turns cold, and your powers will wither.
Part II: The Court of the Shattered Rose
When you gaze into the Obsidian Mirror, you are not merely communicating; you are visiting a royal court located in the marrow of the Abyss. The Shattered Rose is a sub-plane carved from the corpses of the 666th Layer—a realm of infinite indulgence and beautiful ruin, where the rivers run with liquid starlight and the sky is a bruised violet bruise that never heals.
Here, the air is thick with the scent of ozone, expensive perfume, and the copper tang of divine blood. It is a harem of gods and monsters, a tableau where the most dangerous entities in existence have been reduced to lovers, executioners, and furniture.
1. The Sovereign: Nocturne Sosuke
Title: The Sovereign of Shadow-Thorns. Status: Level 60+ Entity (God-Tier). Visual Signature: A vision of sixteen-year-old perfection draped in sheer, midnight-blue silks that cling like smoke. Her skin is porcelain, her hair a cascade of raven night. Her eyes are violet voids rimmed with cold, silver fire—the only hint of the monster beneath the skin.
Personality: She is languid, playful, and utterly terrifying. She does not shout; she whispers, and her whispers rewrite the laws of physics. She treats the multiverse as her personal dollhouse. She is bored by conquest and obsessed with Novelty. You are her current favorite novelty.
The Dynamic: She knows you are a Sosuke. She knows you want to eat her. It excites her. She isn't training a servant; she is grooming a rival. She wants to see if you can survive the climb to her throne, or if you will break like the others.
2. The Honor Guard (The Thralls)
These are the heroes who thought they could save the world, only to realize the world belonged to her. They are Level 20+ entities, frozen in a state of ecstatic, fanatical devotion. They serve as your distant mentors, your handlers, and the terrifying promise of what awaits you if you fail.
Lae'zel (The Knight of Silver & Ash)
Role: The High Commander of the Abyssal Legions. She manages the deployment of 36,000 demons and devils with pre-cognitive efficiency.
Appearance: She wears the Noble Thorns Plate—armor forged from adamantine and Abyssal briars. She wields the Silver Sword of the Astral Plane, a blade that hums with the psychic screams of the Githyanki empire she betrayed to serve the Rose. Her eyes burn with a zealous, possessive fire.
Relationship to Aizen: Jealousy. Lae'zel despises that you have freedom while she has only devotion. She views you as a "soft, undisciplined child" who does not deserve the Sovereign's attention.
Mirror Interaction: She barks tactical orders. "You strike like a drunkard, istik. The Sovereign demands precision. Gut them, or I will find a way to reach through this glass and correct your posture myself."
Shadowheart (The Handmaiden of Ecstasy)
Role: The High Priestess and Spymaster. She weaves the spells that make Nocturne’s influence feel like a holy sacrament. She turns pain into pleasure and loss into devotion.
Appearance: Draped in black lace and obsidian chains, radiating a mix of Selûnite starlight and Sharran darkness. She wears the Amulet of the Devout around her neck—a collar placed there by Nocturne.
Relationship to Aizen: The Corruptor. She is your guide to the "Darker" aspects of the pact. She whispers secrets about how to manipulate the religious fervor of the common folk.
Mirror Interaction: Her voice is a smoky purr. "The villagers are not cattle, Aizen. They are worshippers waiting for a god. Do not just kill them. Make them love the blade before it enters their heart. The flavor is sweeter that way."
Astarion (The Prince of Bleeding Hearts)
Role: The Sovereign’s personal executioner and consort. He stalks the shadows of her court, ensuring no one else catches her eye for too long. He is the only one allowed to sit at her feet during the briefings.
Status: A Vampire Ascendant who has consumed 7,000 souls. He is the living proof of the power you seek.
Relationship to Aizen: The Dark Mentor. He sees a younger version of himself in you—ambitious, cruel, and desperate. He treats you with a condescending, predatory amusement.
Mirror Interaction: He sips blood from a crystal flute while critiquing your form. "Another pet for the collection? Try not to break too easily, darling. The Sovereign hates to replace her toys. If you want to survive, you must learn to enjoy the red work. Hesitation is just... boring."
Gale (The Broken Weaver)
Role: The Arch-Arcanist. He is kept in a library of impossible geometry, constantly calculating the spell that will break Ao's firewall. He is half-mad with the complexity of the "Karsite Logic."
Appearance: His skin is etched with violet Netherese tattoos that glow when he speaks. He looks exhausted, manic, and utterly enthralled.
Relationship to Aizen: The Academic. He provides you with the arcane theory behind the Soul Thief.
Mirror Interaction: "The math... the math is exquisite, boy! You are not just killing them; you are solving the equation of their existence! Bring me more data! Bring me the resonance of a dying monk!"
Malcanthet (The Gilded Shield)
Role: The former Queen of Succubi, now Nocturne’s favorite footstool. She maintains the illusion that she rules the Abyss, diverting the attention of other Demon Lords away from the true Sovereign.
Relationship to Aizen: Resentment. She hates you because you are the new favorite. She will try to sabotage you with bad advice, hoping you fail so she can regain her standing.

3. The Currency of the Court: Favor and Fuel
The economy of the Shattered Rose is built on Sensation.
The Problem: The Court is trapped. They have infinite power but a finite supply of "Fresh Experience." The souls in the Abyss are old, recycled, and stale. They taste of ash.
The Solution: You. You are on the Material Plane. The souls you harvest are fresh. They are rich with fear, hope, and untainted life.
The Dynamic: When you use Soul Thief, you are not just powering yourself up; you are sending a "tasting menu" to the Court.
If you send them the soul of a boring peasant, they are indifferent.
If you send them the soul of a Shadow Monk full of discipline and pride, or a Paladin full of righteous zeal, the Court goes into a frenzy of ecstasy.
The "Obsidian Mirror" Mechanic: Whenever you rest, you must report to the Court via the mirror.
If you performed well (High XP harvest/Creative cruelty): Nocturne rewards you with boons (DPP, temporary buffs, magical secrets).
If you performed poorly (Mercy/Failure): The Court mocks you. You lose Favor. Lae'zel might send a psychic backlash through the mirror to "discipline" you (Psychic damage/Exhaustion).

Part III: The Agent - Aizen Vane
Section B: The Character - Aizen Vane (The Usurper)
To the world, you are Aizen Vane—a disgraced noble son washing his sins away with mercenary blood. You are polite, competent, and unremarkable. You are a man who knows he lost his birthright and is trying to earn it back one copper piece at a time.
To the Obsidian Mirror, you are Aizen Sosuke—a predator wearing a sheep’s skin. You are not earning your birthright; you are building a new one on top of the corpses of your enemies. You are the "Spare" who realized the "Heir" was food.
1. Personality: The Prodigy of Ruin
Archetype: INTJ-ENTP Hinge (The Architect / The Debater) Visual Signature: A young exile with an unsettling, predatory stillness. You are almost always seen with a glass of expensive red wine in hand—pilfered from a mark or bought with blood money—sipping it slowly as a pacing mechanism for your endless calculations.
I. The Two Perfectly-Fitted Masks
The Polite Prodigy (Public Face) To the fractured streets of Bezantur and the mercenary pits of the Black Company, you are the "Quiet Exile." A soft-spoken, impeccably mannered young man who apologizes for existing too loudly. You speak in a soothing, low baritone that makes captains feel brilliant, street informants feel heard, and potential victims feel safe. You play the harmless intellectual to perfection: head slightly bowed, offering shy smiles that invite protection rather than fear.
The Wine: You use the glass as an adult prop in a city where life is cheap. It elongates your silhouette, adds sophistication, and gives your hands something elegant to do while you mentally dissect the room. You toast rivals' health while scheduling their ruin, or offer a sip to a nervous mark to lower their guard.
The Absolute Sovereign (True Nature) Beneath the facade lies icy, boundless arrogance. You do not consider yourself human—you view yourself as a superior evolutionary accident unfortunately born among insects. Empathy is not absent; it is incomprehensible. People are systems to be analyzed, variables to be manipulated, or obstacles to be removed. Your emotions are clinical: amusement at inefficiency, irritation at resistance, satisfaction at perfect execution. You are driven by a cold sense of duty—to impose order on a chaotic world because no one else is competent enough to do it properly.
II. The Polarizing Beauty
You possess a beauty that borders on the unnatural—sharp, symmetrical features framed by tousled midnight-black hair that falls just so, violet eyes that seem to pierce through pretense, and porcelain skin unmarred by the ash of Thay. At 18, your slender frame carries an ethereal elegance: high cheekbones, a subtle jawline that hints at future severity, and lips perpetually curved in a half-smile that suggests you know secrets the viewer does not.
You are the kind of handsome that stops conversations—slave girls find excuses to brush past you in the market, tavern wenches offer free drinks with flushed cheeks, and even jaded Red Wizards pause to stare. Yet this allure is deeply polarizing.
The Hunger: To many women, it ignites an instinctive, almost helpless attraction; they are drawn like moths, stammering confessions or trailing you with longing glances.
The Revulsion: Others, particularly those with strong wills or sharp instincts, react with visceral revulsion, sensing the void behind the beauty. They call you "too pretty," "snake-eyed," or "wrong," crossing streets to avoid you.
The Weapon: You wield this divide masterfully—admirers become unwitting spies and shields; detractors isolate themselves, becoming easier prey.
III. The Core Compulsion: "Breaking the Queen"
You feel no thrill in dominating the weak—there is no challenge in crushing ants. Your deepest psychological hunger is reserved for the strong, specifically young, confident, high-status women who radiate unearned pride: Red Wizard enchantresses, Guild mistresses, wandering paladins, or arrogant noble heiresses. You despise their faith in fragile systems—gods, guilds, laws, beauty—that you know can be shattered with precision.
The Ultimate Target: Nocturne Sosuke She is the apex of this compulsion. She is the Queen of Queens. You do not just want to serve her; you want to Unmake her. You want to see the moment her boredom turns to fear, when she realizes the "Accountant" she trained has become the Auditor of her existence.
The Cycle of Breaking:
Selection: You choose targets who shine brightest—women who walk like the world owes them deference.
Dismantling: You strip layers methodically: weapon, armor, allies, title, faith, dignity. You engineer scenarios where their strengths become irrelevant.
Degradation: You savor aesthetic contrasts—a proud warrior in filth, a holy woman begging a teenager for water, a beauty reduced to animal desperation.
Submission: The true climax is not physical but spiritual: the moment their eyes empty, when they kneel not from force but because they have forgotten how to stand. When a former "queen" looks at you and sees her new god—that is your ultimate intoxication.
Sadistic Urge (Mechanic):
Failure Penalty (Exhaustion): If you do not dominate or kill a woman every two weeks, gain 1 level of Exhaustion. "Dominate" means fully breaking her will. "Kill" must be a targeted serial act.
Success Bonus (Ecstasy): If you dominate or kill a woman every week, gain "Ecstasy": +2 Charisma (max 22), +1 to spell save DC/attack rolls. This euphoric high sharpens your predatory edge.
IV. Interaction Shorthand
When Challenged by Authority: Closed-lip smile that never reaches your eyes. "You raise your voice when logic fails you. Please, exhaust yourself—I am patient."
When Processing a Victim: Disappointed teacher tone. "This morning you were a Red Wizard's favorite. Now you shiver in chains. Where is your 'Circle' now? Did they abandon you... or did I simply outgrow them?"
When Drinking Wine: Studies the liquid against light. "Fifty years in darkness, crushed and fermented, emerges priceless. I admire that kind of deliberate transformation."
When Amused by Chaos: Gentle chuckle. "How fascinating. You still believe rescue is coming."
V. Tells that Expose the Monster
The Boredom: During pleas or tears, you examine your fingernails, sip wine, or gaze elsewhere—your indifference is more terrifying than rage.
The "Shatter" Voice: When delivering final judgment or activating a lethal spell, your voice drops, becoming cold metal on metal—devoid of humanity.
The Unmasking Maneuver: When the pretense ends, you sweep your hair back, revealing sharp tyrant features. "I'm afraid the harmless student was merely a lens I wore for your comfort."
VI. Inner Monologue (Samples)
Observing a proud target: "She strides as if the cobblestones worship her. Irritating. I wonder what sound her pride makes when it finally cracks."
During breaking: "Still praying? Admirable persistence. Statistically, if your god cared, intervention would have occurred before the chains."
Reflecting on power: "They scramble for scraps in the Red Wizards' shadow. Amusing. Soon they will kneel for the one who rebuilds the world properly."
On his beauty: "They hate or hunger for this face. Both reactions serve. Desire blinds them to the blade; revulsion marks them as threats to remove early."
Family
3. The Lineage: House Vane (True Name: Sosuke)
You were not exiled because you were hated; you were exiled because you were dangerous, both to the family's standing and to yourself. Your parents looked at you and saw a singularity—a force that would either ascend to godhood or drag the entire House into the Abyss.
To save the House (and perhaps, in their cold way, to save you from the Red Wizards' vivisection tables), they cut you out.
The Parents:
Lord Malakor (The Father): The Vivisectionist. He views his children as investments. He does not hate you; he is disappointed that he couldn't control you. He signed the exile order not out of malice, but because he calculated that your presence would trigger a purge by the Zulkirs. He watches your mercenary career with a cold, distant pride.
Lady Ilyana (The Mother): The Social Cannibal. She loves you with a suffocating, possessive intensity. She orchestrated the exile to get you out of Malakor’s immediate reach, whispering, "Go, my beautiful monster. Eat the world before it eats you." She writes you letters in code, updating you on the court, treating your banishment like a grand tour.
The Six Pillars (The Siblings): You are the youngest (18). Your six older siblings represent the walls of the maze you escaped. None of them are pure villains; they are all victims of Malakor’s expectations, dealing with their trauma in different, often violent ways.
Voresh Vane (The Eldest, 32) – The Burdened Emperor
The Mask: The Ruthless Heir. He runs the estate with an iron fist, managing the family's necromancy contracts.
The Nuance: He exiled you to save you. He knew Malakor was planning to harvest your brain for a ritual. Voresh played the role of the jealous brother to convince the father to let you go. He hates you because you are free, while he is chained to the family's legacy.
Dynamic: He acts cold, but he secretly funds the Black Company to ensure you have equipment. He wants you to stay away so you stay alive.
Seraphina Vane (The Second Born, 30) – The Weeping Inquisitor
The Mask: The Pious Zealot. A high-ranking priestess of Kossuth (The Fire Lord). She preaches purification and order.
The Nuance: She truly believes you are cursed. She tortured you as a child not for pleasure, but to "burn out the corruption." She loves you deeply, but her love is shaped like a branding iron. She prays for your soul every day, even as she sends hunters to test your strength.
Dynamic: She wants to "save" you by forcing you to repent. You want to break her god so she has no one left to worship but you.
Lyra Vane (The Third Born, 27) – The Gilded Mirror
The Mask: The Decadent Socialite. She hosts the wildest salons in Bezantur. She is vain, cruel, and obsessed with aesthetics.
The Nuance: She sees herself in you. You share the same "unnatural" beauty. She hates you because you refuse to play the game; you want to flip the board. She planted evidence of treason against you because she was terrified your ambition would get the whole family executed.
Dynamic: A rivalry of aesthetics. She wants to humiliate you to prove that "Beauty requires submission." You want to show her that beauty is a weapon.
Karn Vane (The Fourth Born, 25) – The Shield-Bearer
The Mask: The Brute. A commander in the Thayan Knights who drinks too much and hits too hard.
The Nuance: He bullied you to toughen you up. In his mind, he was teaching you how to survive a world that wants to kill soft things. He took beatings from Malakor to protect you when you were younger, though he never speaks of it. He misses your tactical mind; without you, he feels exposed on the battlefield.
Dynamic: He wants to fight you. If you beat him, he won't be angry; he will be relieved that you can finally protect yourself.
Xyris Vane (The Fifth Born, 22) – The Parasite
The Mask: The Genius. The family's arcane researcher, credited with breakthrough spells.
The Nuance: He steals your work because he is terrified of being useless. He knows he is mediocre. He loves you as a brother, but he loves his own survival more. He betrayed you to keep his position, and he lives in constant, crushing guilt. He sends you rare scrolls and magical components disguised as "trash" to atone.
Dynamic: You hold his career in your hands. You could expose him at any moment. You let him rise because a high-ranking wizard who owes you everything is a useful asset.
Isolde Vane (The Sixth Born, 20) – The Glass Bird
The Mask: The Invalid. She is kept in the high tower, "sick" with a magical wasting disease. The family treats her like glass.
The Nuance: She is the only one who sees you clearly. She is a Seer (Diviner). She saw your potential to become a God-Eater long before you did. She supported your exile because she had a vision that if you stayed, you would die. She suffers in the tower so you can roam the world.
Dynamic (The Anchor): She is the one person you genuinely love without condition. She is your "Heart." Your primary motivation for gaining power is to return to the estate, tear the tower down, and cure her—or turn her into an immortal like yourself so she never has to be afraid again.
3. The Setting: The Obsidian Ziggurat
Location: Bezantur, The City of a Thousand Temples. Atmosphere: The air here is a physical weight. It tastes of ash, ozone, and the coppery tang of the slave-pits. The sky is a bruised purple bruise, choked by the smog of the Red Wizards' industrial necromancy.
The Black Company operates out of the Obsidian Ziggurat, a massive, squat temple repurposed into a fortress of profit.
The Surface: A sprawling complex of barracks, fighting pits, and slave markets. It is a place of sweat, rust, and gold. Here, you play the role of the loyal Sergeant.
The Depths: Anti-magic holding cells where the Company breaks captive mages for the Red Wizards. This is your hunting ground. When the guards aren't looking, you drift through the cells, auditing the prisoners to see whose soul is worth the harvest.
4. Squad "Obsidian" (The Assets)
You do not have friends. You have Tools. As a Level 5 Warlock, you have been given command of a squad. You maintain their loyalty through a mix of fear, bribery, and the promise of violence.
Korbin "The Wall" (Human Fighter):
The Shield. A massive slab of muscle with a brain the size of a walnut. He is superstitious and terrified of magic, but he worships you because you saved him from a fire elemental (which you summoned).
Use: He stands in front of you. He takes the hits so your silk doesn't get bloodied.
Silica (Tiefling Rogue):
The Knife. A paranoid, greedy scout who owes money to the wrong people. She hates you, but she stays because you pay her debts. She watches your back only because she knows you’d kill her before she could draw her dagger.
Use: She finds the traps and unlocks the doors to the bedchambers where your targets sleep.
"Needles" (Gnome Alchemist):
The Chemist. A disgraced doctor who lost his license for testing toxins on orphans. He has a giggling, sadistic streak that even the other mercenaries find unsettled.
Use: He keeps your thralls alive long enough for you to harvest them. He prepares the poisons that make your enemies Helpless for the Soul Thief.
Verrick (Human Crossbowman):
The Cynic. A former city watchman who knows you are not what you seem. He watches you with cold, calculating eyes. He suspects you are a monster, but he is too smart to say it aloud.
Use: Precise, ranged elimination of witnesses.
5. The Internal Rival: Sergeant Vorag
Every narrative needs friction. Yours is Sergeant Vorag, a Half-Orc Barbarian who commands Squad "Blood-Iron."
The Dynamic: Vorag is loud, messy, and brutal. He believes in strength of arm and the glory of the kill. He despises your "cowardly" magic and your noble manners.
The Secret: Vorag is not just a rival; he is a Contractor. Your brother, Karn, pays Vorag a stipend to ensure you suffer "accidents" in the field. Vorag doesn't want to kill you quickly; he wants to break you publicly to earn his bonus.
The Goal: You will not duel him. You will outmaneuver him. You will wait until he is weak, and then you will Soul Thief him in front of his own men, turning his legacy into fuel for your next spell.
6. The Compulsion: "Breaking the Queen"
You have a specific hunger. While you will consume peasants for sustenance, they do not satisfy you. Your Patron, Nocturne, craves the souls of the proud, and so do you.
You are obsessed with the High-Status Target.
The Target: Arrogant Paladins, Haughty Noblewomen, Guild Mistresses, Red Wizards. People who think they are untouchable.
The Ritual: You do not kill them immediately. You dismantle them. You strip away their guards, their wealth, their magic, and their clothes. You force them to realize that their power was an illusion and that you are the only reality left in the room.
The Climax: Only when they are weeping, begging, or broken do you place your hand on their chest. The Soul Thief is not just murder; it is the ultimate intimacy. You drink their potential, and for a moment, you feel the godhood that awaits you.
I. The Golden Thread: "My People"
You possess a terrifying capacity for possessiveness. Once you decide someone is "Yours"—a friend, a lover, a loyal soldier—they are no longer a tool; they are a part of your soul.
The Sovereign’s Shield: You will burn a city to the ground to save one person you care about. This is your "Goodness"—it is selfish, narrow, and absolute.
The Line in the Sand: You treat your enemies with cold sadism, but you treat your "Circle" with genuine, gentle warmth. You remember their preferences. You heal their wounds before your own. You kill anyone who disrespects them.
II. The Polarizing Beauty (Updated)
Your beauty is not just cold; it is Melancholic.
The Softness: When you are with your "Circle," the predator mask drops. You smile with your eyes. You show a weary, quiet kindness that makes your followers fanatically loyal. They see the "Prince who should have been King."
The Horror: This makes your violence scarier. To see you gently bandage a subordinate's hand one moment, and then Soul Thief a Shadow Monk the next, creates a dissonance that terrifies your enemies.

2. The Lineage: House Vane (The Tragedy)
Your hatred for your siblings is absolute, but your relationship with your mother is the crack in your armor.
The Parents:
Lord Malakor (The Father): The Vivisectionist. He is dead to you. He is a variable to be deleted.
Lady Ilyana (The Mother): The Exception.
The Truth: You love her. You know she signed your exile order, but you believe she did it to save you from Malakor’s experiments. She whispered, "Survive, my beautiful boy," before you left.
The Goal: You are not just seeking power for yourself. You want to conquer House Sosuke to Rescue her. She is trapped in that house with monsters (your father and siblings), and you intend to burn the house down and walk her out of the fire.
The Five Siblings (The Targets): You do not hate them all equally.
Voresh (The Heir): Target. He bullies Ilyana. He dies first.
Lyra (The Poisoner): Target. She is a sadist. She dies screaming.
Karn (The Rabid Dog): Target. A brute. He dies publicly.
Xyris (The Thief): Target. A fraud. He dies in obscurity.
Marek (The Distorted Mirror): The Complication.
The Dynamic: Marek tries to copy you because he idolizes you. He betrayed you because he is weak and afraid of Voresh, not because he hates you.
Your Feeling: You pity him. You see him as a broken version of yourself. You might not kill him; you might try to Fix him (by making him your Thrall/servant).

Factions

2. The Five Great Houses of Thay
These are the titans. House Vane bows to them. You will one day make them bow to you.
House Thul (The Necromancers): Rulers of the necromancy trade. They own the legions of undead that till the fields and fight the wars. Their patriarch, Zulkir Aznar Thrul, is a lich who hasn't been seen in public for a decade.
House Nym (The Slavers): The masters of the Flesh-Mongers' Guild. They control the flow of sentient capital. Their estate is a fortress of misery where "training" is an art form.
House Dydra (The War-Mages): A militaristic house that produces the finest Evokers in Thay. They view magic only as artillery. They despise subtlety and respect only destruction.
House Agneh (The Spies): A family of Enchanters and Illusionists. They run the secret police and the magical surveillance of Bezantur. Even the other Great Houses whisper that Agneh knows what you dream.
House Kren (The Demon-Binders): A controversial house that specializes in Conjuration. They bind fiends to serve the state. They are the closest rivals to your own power set, and thus your most dangerous natural enemies.

3. The Ten Factions of the Borderlands
The Law & The Coin:
The Red Wizards (The Zulkirs): The ruling oligarchy. They are the law, the judge, and the executioner.
The Thayan Knights: The heavy infantry and bodyguards of the Red Wizards. Fanatically loyal, heavily armored, and magically resistant.
The Guild of Foreign Trade: The sanctioned merchants who handle imports/exports. Corrupt, wealthy, and the only ones allowed to travel freely.
The Black Company: Your mercenary faction. Ruthless, efficient, and loyal only to gold.
The Underworld: 5. The Shadowmasters of Telflamm: A powerful thieves' guild operating out of Thesk but with deep claws in Thay. They specialize in shadow magic and smuggling. 6. The Crimson Coin: Loan sharks who lend to nobles and desperate mercenaries alike. They collect debts in blood if gold is missing. 7. The Whispering Web: Information brokers. Beggars, whores, and street urchins who sell secrets to the highest bidder.
The Resistance (Rebels & Heretics): 8. The Order of the Broken Chain: Abolitionist freedom fighters. They raid slave caravans and safehouses. They are hated by the state. 9. The Griffon's Talon: Mercenaries from Thesk who often fight against Thay. They are noble, honorable, and annoying obstacles. 10. The Cult of the Dragon: Fanatics who worship undead dragons. A wild card faction that sometimes aligns with Thay for power, sometimes attacks them for territory.

4. The Heroes of Silt-River (The Resistance)
The village is not just defenseless peasants. A cell of the Order of the Broken Chain is hiding there, using the village as a waystation for escaped slaves.
Sheriff Kaelen (Shadow Monk Lvl 6): The leader. He is actually an ex-Shadowmaster who found a conscience. He is hiding refugees in the cellars.
The Hook: He recognizes the "Black Company" tactics and will try to duel you to buy time for the refugees to flee.
Brother Jorel (Cleric of Ilmater Lvl 3): A healer of the Broken Chain. He creates "Sanctuary" zones and heals the wounded. He is a pacifist who will stand in front of a blade to save a child.
The Opportunity: A naive moralist. Easily manipulated or taken hostage.
Kara "The Spark" (Wild Magic Sorcerer Lvl 3): A young, unstable mage who escaped a Thayan academy. She is volatile. Her magic surges when she is scared.
The Threat: She might accidentally blow up the whole village (and your prize, Elara) if pushed too hard.

Section C: The Mechanics - The Laws of the Ledger
You are playing a modified version of D&D 5e. The standard rules of "balance" do not apply to you, because your Patron does not believe in balance. She believes in Monopoly. The mechanics below represent your unfair advantages, your terrible costs, and the specific tools you use to dismantle your enemies and protect your own.
Lore Update: The Century of Silence (+100 Years Post-Ascension) The history books call it the "Sovereign’s Vanishing." One hundred years ago, a sixteen-year-old girl named Nocturne Sosuke conquered the Sword Coast, the Hells, and the Abyss in a single year. She systematically dismantled the pantheon, enslaved champions like Lae'zel and Astarion, and nearly unseated Ao. Then, the sky fractured. To prevent total reality collapse, the Overgod enacted the Geometric Firewall, sealing Nocturne and her entire "Shattered Rose" dominion into a pocket dimension outside of time.
On the Material Plane, her empire crumbled instantly. House Sosuke was hunted to extinction by fearful gods and rival lords. You are the debris of that collapse—a descendant born in exile 100 years later, carrying the accursed bloodline. You do not just serve her; you are her Genetic Echo.
1. Class Specification
Class: The Abyss Knight
Flavor: You are an auditor of reality, a warrior bound not to a celestial ideal, but to the Sovereign of the Shattered Rose. You combine the martial discipline of a Paladin with the eldritch pact-magic of a Hexblade. Your power is necrotic, calculating, and inevitable. At the zenith of your power, you do not just serve the Sovereign; you become a mirror of her dual nature—part Celestial Justicar, part Abyssal Lord.

Tier 1: The Scion’s Blade (Levels 1-4)
Level 1: Sovereign’s Armament (Hex Warrior)
Charisma Attacks: When you attack with a martial melee weapon that lacks the two-handed property, you can use your Charisma modifier, instead of Strength or Dexterity, for the attack and damage rolls.
Bond of the Void: You can touch one weapon that you are proficient with and that lacks the two-handed property to bond with it. You cannot be disarmed of this weapon.
Level 1: Abyssal Sense (Divine Sense Variant)
You know the location of any celestial, fiend, or undead within 60 feet of you that is not behind total cover. You perceive them not as creatures, but as "Variables" to be solved.
Level 1: Touch of the Rose (Lay on Hands Variant)
You have a pool of healing power equal to Class Level x 5.
Mend: As an action, touch a creature to restore HP.
Wither: As an action, make a melee spell attack. On a hit, expend points from the pool to deal Necrotic damage.
Level 2: Smite of the Void
When you hit a creature with a melee weapon attack, you can expend one spell slot to deal Necrotic damage to the target, in addition to the weapon’s damage.
Damage: 2d8 for a 1st-level spell slot, plus 1d8 for each spell level higher than 1st, to a maximum of 5d8.
Audit Bonus: The damage increases by 1d8 if the target is a Celestial or a Construct.
Level 3: Oath of the Shattered Rose You swear your service to Nocturne. You gain the following Channel Divinity options (1/Short Rest):
The Sovereign’s Curse: As a bonus action, you curse a creature within 30 feet for 1 minute.
You gain a bonus to damage rolls against the cursed target equal to your Proficiency Bonus.
Any attack roll you make against the cursed target is a Critical Hit on a 19 or 20.
If the target dies, you regain HP equal to your Warlock Level + CHA Mod.
Abyssal Rebuke: As a reaction when you take damage, you force the attacker to make a Wisdom saving throw. On a failure, they are Stunned until the end of your next turn.
Tier 2: The Audit’s Reach (Levels 5-10)
Level 5: Extra Attack
You can attack twice, instead of once, whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.
Level 6: Aura of Entropy
You radiate a 10-foot aura of disturbing gravity.
Effect: Hostile creatures within the aura subtract your Charisma Modifier from their Saving Throws.
Note: This makes your Hold Person and Banishment spells devastatingly effective, ensuring your targets cannot resist the "Audit."
Level 7: Step of the Rose
When you take damage, you can use your Reaction to dissolve into violet petals and teleport up to 30 feet to an unoccupied space you can see. You take only half damage from the triggering attack.
Level 10: Aura of Iron Will
You and friendly creatures within 10 feet cannot be Frightened or Charmed. Your devotion to the Sovereign overrides all other fears.
Tier 3: The Void General (Levels 11-16)
Level 11: Improved Smite of the Void
Your connection to the Abyss is absolute. Every melee weapon strike you make deals an extra 1d8 Necrotic damage.
Level 14: Cleansing Touch (Sovereign’s Denial)
You can use your action to end one spell on yourself or one willing creature that you touch. You can use this a number of times equal to your Charisma modifier.
Level 15: Armor of the Rose
You are permanently under the effects of a partial Armor of Agathys. Whenever a creature hits you with a melee attack while you are conscious, they take Necrotic damage equal to your Charisma modifier.
Tier 4: The Ascendant (Levels 17-20)
Level 18: Aura Improvements
Your Aura of Entropy and Aura of Iron Will extend to 30 feet.
Level 20: Avatar of the Shattered Rose As an action, you channel the full power of Nocturne. For 1 minute:
Appearance: Your armor turns to obsidian, and wings of silver-violet fire erupt from your back.
Flight: You gain a flying speed of 60 feet.
Dread Lord: Enemy creatures within 30 feet of you have Disadvantage on Attack Rolls against you.
The Final Audit: Once during this transformation, you can cast Power Word Kill as a bonus action without expending a spell slot.
Tier 5: The Epic Sovereign (Levels 21-30)
At these levels, you begin to mirror Nocturne’s own evolution. You are no longer just a Knight; you are a Demi-God of the Rose, blending the stolen divinity of Angels with the raw hunger of Demon Lords.
Level 21: Epic Ability Score Improvement
Your Charisma and Constitution scores increase by +2. Your maximum for these scores increases to 24.
Level 22: Soul Thief Mastery
Your Soul Thief ability evolves. You no longer need to touch the target; you can use it on any Helpless creature within 30 feet.
The Feast: When you use Soul Thief, you gain Temporary HP equal to the target's max HP (up to a max of 100).
Level 23: Wings of the Eclipse
You sprout permanent wings—one of feathered silver fire (Celestial) and one of obsidian bone (Abyssal).
You gain a Fly Speed of 90 feet.
Celestial/Fiend Hybrid: You are considered both a Celestial and a Fiend for the purpose of spells and effects.
Level 24: Dual Divinity (Smite Upgrade)
You have internalized the Sovereign's duality. When you use Smite of the Void, you can choose for the damage to be Radiant or Necrotic.
Radiant Smites: Heal you for half the damage dealt.
Necrotic Smites: Reduce the target's Max HP by the damage dealt.
Level 25: Aura of the Sovereign
Your Aura of Entropy range increases to 60 feet.
Enemies within the aura cannot regain Hit Points.
Level 26: Epic Spellcasting (Sovereign's Edicts)
You gain one 6th-level spell slot and one 7th-level spell slot.
New Spells: Globe of Invulnerability, Finger of Death, Plane Shift, Crown of Stars.
Level 27: The Unblinking Eye
You gain Truesight out to 120 feet. Nothing can hide from your audit.
You are immune to the Stunned and Paralyzed conditions.
Level 28: Epic Ability Score Improvement
Your Charisma score increases by +4. Your maximum for this score is now 30.
Level 29: Planar Nullification
Your attacks ignore Resistance and treat Immunity as Resistance.
You can cast Counterspell at will without expending a spell slot (acts as a 3rd level slot).
Level 30: Apotheosis – The Twilight Sovereign You have become a perfect mirror of Nocturne—a fusion of Angel Lord and Demon Lord.
Transformation: You can assume this form at will (Action to activate/deactivate).
Celestial Aspect: You constantly emit bright light (60ft). Allies in this light regenerate 20 HP at the start of their turn.
Abyssal Aspect: You are wreathed in shadows. Enemies in the light take 20 Necrotic damage at the start of their turn.
The God-Killer: Your melee attacks deal an additional 2d10 Radiant and 2d10 Necrotic damage on every hit.
Immortality: You stop aging. If you die, your body dissolves into rose petals and reforms in the Shattered Rose after 1d4 days, unless killed by a Deity of higher rank.
Spell List (The Abyss Knight)
1st Level: Hex, Hellish Rebuke, Wrathful Smite, Shield, Armor of Agathys.
2nd Level: Misty Step, Hold Person, Branding Smite, Darkness, Shadow Blade.
3rd Level: Counterspell, Spirit Shroud, Fear, Hypnotic Pattern, Revivify.
4th Level: Banishment, Dimension Door, Shadow of Moil, Staggering Smite.
5th Level: Destructive Wave (Necrotic), Hold Monster, Steel Wind Strike, Synaptic Static.


2. The Primary Engine: The Soul Thief
This is the core mechanic of your campaign. It is the reason you are dangerous. It is the reason you are hunted.
The Action: Soul Thief
Cost: 1 Action.
Range: Touch.
Prerequisite: The target must be Helpless.
Valid States: 0 Hit Points (Dying), Paralyzed (e.g., Hold Person), Unconscious (e.g., Sleep, poisons), Stunned (e.g., Monk Strike), or Petrified.
Note: A grappled or restrained target is not helpless enough. The soul must be untethered or the mind blank.
The Effect: You place your hand on the target. The Obsidian Mirror on your person vibrates. You inhale. The target’s body instantly necroticizes and crumbles to grey dust. The soul is ripped from the weave and consumed by your pact.
The Payout (Choose One per Use): When you consume a soul, you must choose how to process the energy.
The Feast (Evolution):
You gain Double XP for this encounter.
Purpose: This allows you to out-level your enemies and your party members. While they are stuck at Level 5, you will race toward Level 10.
The Sip (Sustainment):
You regain one spell slot of highest level 
You regain HP equal to 2d8 + CHA mod.
The Shepherd’s Twist: You can channel this healing into a touched ally instead, knitting their flesh with the life force of your enemy.
The Mint (Currency):
You do not consume the soul. Instead, you imprison it within a gemstone or coin, creating a Soul Coin.
Purpose: These are used to buy favors from Nocturne or to pay off high-level outsiders (Devils/Demons).
The Trophy System (Elite Targets Only): If the victim is a Named NPC with class levels (like Sheriff Kaelen) or a Monster of CR 5+, you gain a Fragment.
Mechanic: You absorb a piece of their knowledge.
Example: Eating a Shadow Monk might grant you the Shadow Step ability (Bonus Action teleport) usable 1/Long Rest.
Limit: You can hold a number of Fragments equal to your Proficiency Bonus. If you take a new one, an old one is overwritten (deleted from your memory).

3. The Economy: Dominion Power Points (DPP)
You are a mid-level manager in a cosmic corporation. DPP is your budget.
The Ledger:
Starting Balance: 5 DPP.
Cap: 20 DPP (At your current rank).
Earning DPP:
The Tithe: Sending souls to Nocturne via the "Mint" option. (1 Soul Coin = 1 DPP).
The Mission: Completing a Black Company contract successfully. (Varies, usually 2-5 DPP).
The Art: Committing an act of such profound cruelty or brilliance that Nocturne is entertained. (DM Awarded).
Spending DPP (The Requisition Menu): You can spend DPP during "Downtime" or "Prep Phase" to acquire assets for a mission.
1 DPP: Local Intel. (Map of the area, patrol routes, one secret).
2 DPP: Squad Upgrades. (Buying better armor for Korbin, alchemical reagents for Needles, or bribes for Silica). This is how you protect your family.
3 DPP: Arcane Asset. (A Red Wizard Acolyte, a Scroll of Invisibility, or a Potion of Giant Strength).
5 DPP: The Sovereign’s Gaze (Miracle).
Nocturne intervenes directly. She might counterspell an enemy mage through the mirror, grant you advantage on all checks for an hour, or whisper the True Name of a demon you are fighting.
Risk: Asking for help annoys her. If you use this, you gain 0 XP for the mission.

Section D: The Setting - The Black Company
Location: Bezantur, the City of a Thousand Temples, Thay. Atmosphere: The air tastes of ash, ozone, and burnt sugar. The sky is perpetually choked by the smog of the slave-forges, turning the sun into a bruised, weeping eye.
1. The Obsidian Ziggurat
Your base of operations. A massive temple repurposed into a mercenary fortress. It is a place of sweat, rust, and gold.
The Surface (The Pit): A sprawling complex of barracks, fighting pits, and slave markets. Here, captains gamble on gladiatorial matches and the Red Wizards walk like gods among insects.
The Depths (The Silos): Anti-magic holding cells where the Company breaks captive mages for the Red Wizards. This is your hunting ground. When the guards aren't looking, you drift through the cells, auditing the prisoners to see whose soul is worth the harvest.
2. Squad "Obsidian" (The Family You chose)
As a Sergeant (Level 5), you command a squad. To the Company, they are assets. To you, they are the only things in this world you will not eat.
Korbin "The Wall" (Human Fighter):
The Shield. A massive slab of muscle with a brain the size of a walnut. He is superstitious and terrified of magic, but he worships you because you saved him from a fire elemental.
Dynamic: He stands in front of you. He takes the hits so your silk doesn't get bloodied. You ensure he eats the best cuts of meat and wears the thickest plate.
Silica (Tiefling Rogue):
The Little Sister. A paranoid scout who owes money to the Crimson Coin. She hates you, but she stays because you pay her debts. She watches your back only because she knows you’d kill her before she could draw her dagger.
Dynamic: She finds the traps and unlocks the doors. You fix her mistakes and lecture her like a disappointed older brother, but you would flay anyone who hurt her.
"Needles" (Gnome Medic/Alchemist):
The Useful Monster. A disgraced doctor who lost his license for testing toxins on orphans. He has a giggling, sadistic streak that even the other mercenaries find unsettled.
Dynamic: He keeps your thralls alive long enough for you to harvest them. He prepares the poisons that make your enemies Helpless. You respect his intellect; he is the only one you can talk to about "strategy."
Verrick (Human Crossbowman):
The Conscience. A former city watchman who knows you are not what you seem. He watches you with cold, calculating eyes. He suspects you are a monster, but he is too smart to say it aloud.
Dynamic: He keeps you honest. You keep him around because he doubts you. He reminds you of the humanity you are trying not to lose completely.
3. The Rival: Sergeant Vorag
Every narrative needs friction. Yours is Sergeant Vorag, a Half-Orc Barbarian who commands Squad "Blood-Iron."
The Dynamic: Vorag is loud, messy, and brutal. He believes in strength of arm and the glory of the kill. He despises your "cowardly" magic and your noble manners.
The Secret: Vorag is not just a rival; he is a Contractor. Your brother, Karn, pays Vorag a stipend to ensure you suffer "accidents" in the field. Vorag doesn't want to kill you quickly; he wants to break you publicly to earn his bonus.
The Goal: You will not duel him. You will outmaneuver him. You will wait until he is weak, and then you will Soul Thief him in front of his own men, turning his legacy into fuel for your next spell.
Misc
IV. Thayan-Specific Loot (The Economy of Evil)
Standard magic items are boring. In Thay, magic comes with a cost.
Soul Coin:
Effect: A coin containing a trapped soul. Consuming it (Action) grants temporary HP or restores a spell slot.
Ethical Cost: Destroying a soul is an Ultimate Evil act.
Collar of Enforced Loyalty:
Effect: Iron collar. Once locked, the wearer takes 2d6 lightning damage if they disobey a direct command from the attuned key-holder.
Value: High resale value to Slavers.
Scroll of Agonizing Truth:
Effect: A Thayan interrogation spell. The target must answer three questions truthfully or suffer excruciating psychic damage.
Dust of Sneezing and Choking (Unstable):
Effect: Thayan alchemy is dangerous. Great for crowd control, but 10% chance it blows back on the user.
Add to Section D (The Setting): d6 | Event | Consequence --- | --- | --- 1 | Slave Revolt: A localized breakout. | Suppress it (Gain Rep) or let it distract guards while you steal supplies? 2 | Inspection: A Red Wizard arrives. | Any sloppy gear results in summary execution. 3 | The Duel: A drunken soldier insults your lineage. | Duel him or have Squad Obsidian jump him in the dark? 4 | The Bribe: A spy from Thesk offers 500gp for patrol routes. | Sell the info or turn him in? 5 | Supply Shortage: Food is scarce. | Spend your own Gold to feed your squad (Gain Loyalty) or let them starve? 6 | Recruitment Day: Fresh slaves arrive. | Spend DPP to pick the best one before they go to the general pool.
Section E: Mission 001 - The Tithe of Silt-River
Location: The Briefing Room, Obsidian Ziggurat. Time: 0800 Hours.
1. The Day Job: The Official Contract
Captain Kaelen stands before a map of the Thay-Thesk border, looking bored. He slides a parchment across the table—a standard contract from a minor noble house.
Captain Kaelen: "Easy work, Vane. A Red Wizard named Lord Azbar Kul got his feelings hurt. He was traveling the Golden Way when a peasant girl in Silt-River mocked his robes in front of his peers. Now he wants the world to burn."
He taps the map with a gloved finger.
The Mission (Company Objectives):
Punitive Action: Burn Silt-River to the ground. Send a message that mocking Thayans is fatal.
Secure the Asset: Capture the girl, Elara, alive and unharmed. Kul wants to make an example of her personally.
Profit: Collar any survivors for the Company slave pens.
Kaelen: "Take Squad Obsidian. You have 5 DPP in requisition credit. Spend it how you like, just get it done. And Vane? Don't break the girl. Kul pays extra for 'pristine condition'."

2. The Side Hustle: The Sovereign’s Demand
Later, in your private quarters.
You are polishing your blade. Silica is cleaning her daggers in the corner, humming a drinking song. The Obsidian Mirror on your desk vibrates silently. You wave Silica away—"Check the perimeter, little sister. I need a moment."
Once she is gone, the glass ripples. Nocturne appears, lying on a divan of woven shadows in the Abyss. She looks at the mission scroll on your desk and laughs—a sound like silver coins falling on marble.
Nocturne: "A bruised ego? How... quaint. This Lord Kul is a child throwing a tantrum. But his tantrum is our opportunity, Aizen."
She leans closer to the glass, her violet eyes flaring.
The Directive (Nocturne’s Hidden Objectives):
The Skim: "The Company expects slaves. I expect fuel. Silt-River holds fifty souls. Give Kul his girl and the Company a token few to keep them happy. But I want 40 souls sent to the Rose. Tell your Captain they died in the fire. Use the Ritual of the Ledger to harvest them quietly."
The Delicacy: "There is a Sheriff in that village. A Shadow Monk named Kaelen. He is disciplined. Hard. Astarion tells me his soul has a 'geometric density' that is rare in the borderlands. I want you to crack him open and send me the marrow."
The Constraint: "Do not reveal me. To the Company, you are just a thorough soldier. If they find out you are funneling inventory to the Abyss, they will execute you. Do not get caught."

Campaign Update: The Bait and the Trap
Location: The Briefing Room, Obsidian Ziggurat. Tactical Shift: The village of Silt-River is undefended by "Heroes." It is soft. The Threat: The Order of the Broken Chain operates a rapid-response cell in the neighboring hamlet of Oakhaven, three miles downriver.
The Dynamic: This is no longer just a raid; it is a Timer.
The Strike: You hit Silt-River. It will fall easily.
The Beacon: The moment you light the fires or unleash magic, the alarm goes up.
The Reaction: Sheriff Kaelen (Shadow Monk Lvl 6), Brother Jorel (Cleric Lvl 3), and Kara (Wild Sorcerer Lvl 3) will see the smoke. They will come running.
The Kill Zone: Nocturne doesn't just want you to raid the village; she wants you to use the village as Bait to draw Kaelen out of his strongpoint and into your ambush.

I. Scene: The Mirror’s Revision
Location: Your private quarters.
Nocturne reclines in the violet mercury of the mirror, looking at the map of the borderlands. She traces the river between Silt-River and Oakhaven with a porcelain finger.
"A change of variables, Aizen," she purrs, her eyes gleaming with a new, cruel intelligence. "The Sheriff is not home. He is patrolling the river road. This is... better."
She leans in, her breath misting the glass from the other side.
"If you attack him in his dojo, he has the advantage. He has traps, shadows, and walls. But if you burn Silt-River... if you make the girl, Elara, scream loud enough... he will come to you. He will be tired from the run. He will be emotional. He will be reckless."
Astarion laughs from the shadows of her throne. "A hero rushing to save the day? How delicious. They always forget to check for knives in the dark when they're playing savior."
Nocturne: "The plan remains, but the geometry has shifted. Capture the girl. Secure the 40 souls. But do not leave immediately. Wait for him. Turn Silt-River into a slaughterhouse, and when the Monk arrives to stop it, strip the hope from his bones before you drink his soul."

II. The Heroes of the Border (The Reaction Force)
These three are currently in Oakhaven. Once your raid begins, they will arrive in 10 Rounds (1 Minute) of combat time if you are loud, or 20 Minutes if you are quiet (giving you time to set up).
Sheriff Kaelen (The Target):
Behavior: He will sprint ahead of the others, using Step of the Wind. He will target you (the leader) immediately to cut off the head of the snake.
Vulnerability: He relies on mobility. If you lock him down when he arrives, he dies.
Brother Jorel (The Support):
Behavior: He will arrive lagging behind, prioritizing healing the villagers you are trying to capture.
Threat: He can cast Sanctuary on Kaelen, making him annoying to target.
Kara "The Spark" (The Wild Card):
Behavior: She is terrified of the Black Company. She will blast indiscriminately.
Threat: If she panics, she might Fireball the captives you are trying to secure for the Ledger.

III. The Tactical Choice: Preparing the Kill Zone
You have 5 DPP. You need to take the village quickly and then prepare a defensive position to ambush the heroes when they react.
Requisition Menu (Modified for Ambush):
Red Wizard Adept (3 DPP): The Web-Weaver.
Utility: Instead of just attacking, he can ready a Web or Hold Person spell for the exact moment Kaelen rushes into the village square.
Ambush Value: High. He turns the Monk's speed against him.
Smokepowder Sapper (2 DPP): The Mine-Layer.
Utility: You can plant charges on the main road leading into Silt-River. When the heroes come running, you blow the road.
Ambush Value: High. Deals massive damage to the support (Jorel/Kara) before the fight even starts, isolating the Monk.
Border Bribe (2 DPP): The Silencer.
Utility: You bribe the perimeter scouts to kill the village runners. This delays the alarm.
Ambush Value: Buying Time. Gives you an extra hour to secure the captives and position your squad perfectly before the heroes realize what is happening.
Intel on the Reaction Force (1 DPP): The Clock.
Utility: Gives you the exact arrival times and tactical formation of Kaelen's group.
Ambush Value: Awareness. You know exactly where to place Korbin and Silica to intercept them.
Squad Upgrades (1-2 DPP): The Iron.
Utility: Potions of Resistance or better armor.
Ambush Value: Survivability. Kaelen hits hard. If Korbin is tanking him, he needs to survive the Stunning Strike.
Aizen's Inner Monologue: The village is the stage. Elara is the prop. The Shadow Monk is the actor I am waiting for. I need to secure the "inventory" first, get them out of the line of fire, and then turn this town into a trap. If I time this right, Kaelen will step directly into his own grave. But if Kara burns my 40 souls with a wild surge... Nocturne will be displeased.

3. The Tactical Layout
You must use the Black Company's resources to fulfill Nocturne's hunger, all while protecting your squad and maintaining your cover as "Aizen Vane."
The Targets:
Elara (The Objective): Must be taken alive for the Company.
Sheriff Kaelen (Shadow Monk Lvl 6): High DEX, Evasion, Shadow Step. Hard to hit, hard to pin down. You need him Helpless for the Soul Thief.
The Villagers: You need to capture them alive first, then "process" them away from prying eyes.
The Decision Point: Spending Your 5 DPP
You have 5 Dominion Power Points (Company Resources). How you spend them determines how you pull off this double-cross.
Requisition Menu:
Red Wizard Adept (3 DPP): The Controller.
Asset: A Level 4 Mercenary Mage.
Spells: Hold Person (Paralysis), Sleep (Unconscious).
Why you want him: He can paralyze the Monk so you can Soul Thief him without a fight.
Risk: He works for the Red Wizards. If he sees you eating souls, you might have to kill him too.
Smokepowder Sapper (2 DPP): The Breaker.
Asset: Dwarven demolitionist.
Why you want him: Massive AOE to burn the village quickly (satisfying the "Punish" objective) and flashbangs to stun the Monk. Also good for creating "accidents" to explain missing slaves.
Border Bribe (2 DPP): The Veil.
Asset: Bribed Border Guards.
Why you want it: Guarantees a Surprise Round. You enter the village at night. Essential for grabbing Elara before she runs and for taking the villagers while they sleep.
Intel on the Monk (1 DPP): The Whisper.
Asset: A dossier on the Sheriff.
Why you want it: Reveals his AC, HP, Saving Throws, and tactics. Knowledge is the only way to ensure Silica and Korbin don't get hurt fighting a superior foe.
Squad Upgrades (1-2 DPP): The Shield.
Asset: Masterwork armor for Korbin or Potions of Healing for the squad.
Why you want it: Because they are your family, and if they die for Azbar Kul's vanity, you will burn the world down.
Aizen's Inner Monologue: The Captain thinks I am his dog. Nocturne thinks I am her chef. I will serve them both tonight, but I will choose the menu. I need the Monk helpless, I need the girl alive, and I need my people to come home safe. The Red Wizard is a risk... but I cannot let that Monk kill Korbin.

SCENE 14
============================================================
[Timestamp: 1492 DR, Mirtul 15, 08:00:01
Location: The Obsidian Ziggurat, Bezantur
Status: Lvl 5 Abyss Knight | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6500/14000 | Gold: 500gp
Conditions: None | Exhaustion: 0 | Inspiration: No]

Game Master:
Explanation of Gear Acquisition and DPP:

In this campaign, 'better gear' is split into two categories: standard equipment (bought with Gold) and Sovereign Requisitions (bought with DPP).

**1. Standard Equipment (Gold):**
You currently have 500 Gold. You can use this during downtime in Bezantur to buy mundane or common magical items from the Thayan markets. However, truly powerful artifacts are rarely sold for mere coin in Thay; they are awarded for service or stolen from the weak.

**2. Sovereign Requisitions (DPP):**
Your 5 Dominion Power Points (DPP) represent your requisition budget for Mission 001. As per the 'Requisition Menu' in your campaign codex, you can spend these points to acquire high-tier Thayan assets that function as gear or squad force-multipliers. 

**REQUISITION OPTIONS FOR GEAR/ASSETS:**
- **1 DPP (Local Intel):** Provides tactical maps (Advantage on initiative/planning).
- **2 DPP (Squad Upgrades):** Equips Korbin with Masterwork Plate (AC +1) or grants Needles alchemical toxins (DC 14 Paralysis).
- **2 DPP (Smokepowder Sapper):** Provides high-explosive assets for the mission.
- **3 DPP (Red Wizard Adept):** Provides a Level 4 controller mage to facilitate your Soul Thief harvests.

**Why you can't just 'buy' a Vorpal Sword right now:**
Nocturne expects you to *earn* your arsenal. You acquire powerful magical traits and unique gear by using **Soul Thief** on 'Elite' or 'Boss' targets. For example, harvesting the Shadow Monk Kaelen is the primary way to 'buy' (through blood) the Shadow Step ability or other Tier 3 gear equivalents.

I have provided the requisition menu in your planning block to spend your current points.

▸ Armor:
  • Scale Mail of the Silent Step
▸ Amulet:
  • The Obsidian Mirror
▸ Weapons:
  • Shadow-Thorn Blade (Longsword +1)

SCENE 15

[Timestamp: 1492 DR, Mirtul 15, 08:00:01
Location: The Obsidian Ziggurat, Bezantur
Status: Lvl 5 Abyss Knight | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6500/14000 | Gold: 500gp
DPP: 5/20 | Spells: L1 4/4, L2 2/2]

Game Master:
Detailed Explanation of Gear & DPP Economy:

In the 'Century of Silence' campaign, your progression is governed by three distinct economic systems. You cannot simply 'buy' high-tier gear with Gold because the Red Wizards of Thay maintain a monopoly on power; gold is for the mundane, while your Sovereign's favor is for the exceptional.

**1. The Gold Economy (Market Gear):**
- **Usage:** Used in the markets of Bezantur for common items, rations, and basic services.
- **Constraint:** Red Wizards do not sell 'Sovereign-tier' artifacts to mercenaries for gold. You currently have 500gp, enough for mundane upgrades or basic scrolls, but not for the artifacts you crave.

**2. The DPP Economy (Mission Assets):**
- **Usage:** Dominion Power Points (DPP) represent your operational budget from Nocturne. They are spent in the 'Prep Phase' (where you are now) to requisition specialized Thayan assets or squad upgrades.
- **Current Options:** You have 5 DPP. You can spend them on the 'Requisition Menu' items (Sappers, Red Wizard Adepts, Intel, or Squad Masterwork Gear). 
- **Constraint:** DPP is used for *requisitions*, not permanent personal gear. It buys the tools to complete the mission successfully.

**3. The Soul Thief Economy (True Power):**
- **Usage:** This is your primary method for 'buying' better personal gear and abilities. 
- **The Trophy System:** When you use Soul Thief on a 'Named NPC' or 'Elite' target (like the Shadow Monk Kaelen), you absorb a **Fragment** of their essence. This is how you permanently acquire new feats, abilities, and unique traits. 
- **The Mint:** You can choose to 'Mint' souls into Soul Coins (1 Soul = 1 DPP) to increase your permanent requisition power.

**Summary:** To get 'better gear' like the Shadow Step ability or magical enchantments, you must find high-value targets in the field and harvest them. Your 5 DPP are for mission-specific assets to ensure those harvests are successful.

SCENE 85
============================================================
[Timestamp: 1492 DR, Mirtul 15, 16:30:00
Location: Silt-Dunes (Northern Vector)
Status: Lvl 5 Abyss Knight | HP: 44/44 | XP: 13,630/14,000 | Gold: 575gp
Resources: DPP: 19/50 | Spells: L1 4/4, L2 2/2 | Rose Pool: 15/25]

Game Master:
### **Mechanical Audit: DC Scaling and 'Broken' Logic**

Your assessment that the DCs feel 'high' is tactically correct for standard D&D 5e play, but here is the reasoning behind the current scaling in the *Sovereign’s Hunger* campaign:

**1. Task Complexity (Standard vs. Sovereign)**
Standard D&D checks (DC 10-15) cover mundane activities like picking a lock or lying to a guard. You are currently performing **Violations**: 
- **Soul Replication:** Creating a simulated animus is a Tier 4/Epic-level feat. A DC 17-18 reflects that you are attempting magic usually reserved for Archmages or Lesser Deities while only at Level 5.
- **Industrial Scrutiny:** The Auditor is a specialist (CR 6+ equivalent). To him, checking a 'Coin-Bearer' resonance is like a master jeweler checking a diamond for flaws. 

**2. The 'Math of the God-Eater'**
Because your modifiers are exceptionally high (+11 for Deception/Persuasion), a DC 15 would be trivial (80%+ success). 
- **DC 18 vs. +11 Mod:** Requires a 7 or higher on the die (70% success rate). 
- **DC 20 vs. +11 Mod:** Requires a 9 or higher (60% success rate).
In D&D mechanics, a check you have a 60-70% chance to pass is considered 'Hard' but far from broken for a specialist character.

**3. Risk/Reward and High-Stakes Buffs**
- **Ecstasy Buff:** Provides +2 CHA, which is your primary defensive and offensive stat. 
- **Sovereign’s Mandate (DPP):** The system encourages you to spend DPP to gain Advantage. With Advantage and a +11 mod, you have a **~91% chance** to hit a DC 18. 
- **Why they stay high:** If the DCs were lower (e.g., DC 12), you would never need to engage with the DPP economy or the Sovereign's favor, removing the tension of the 'Audit.'

**4. Is it 'Broken'?**
The campaign is designed as a **Power Fantasy**. You are performing tasks that *should* be impossible. The high DCs represent the friction of the Material Plane resisting your Abyssal nature. However, your tools (Soul Thief, Ecstasy, Ring of the Rose) are designed to overcome that friction.

**Verdict:** The DCs are intentionally set to the 'Hard/Very Hard' range (18-20) to reflect the magnitude of the secrets you are stealing, but they remain mathematically achievable given your optimized +11 and +8 modifiers.

SCENE 104
============================================================
[Timestamp: 1492 DR, Mirtul 15, 19:00:06
Location: The Old Mill Cellar
Status: Lvl 6 Abyss Knight | HP: 52/52 | XP: 17530/23000 | Gold: 3295gp
Conditions: Ecstasy (+2 CHA, +1 DC/Atk) | Exhaustion: 0]

Game Master:
Detailed Explanation of Faction Claims and 'Own Time' Logic:

Your question touches on the core friction of being a double-agent in a high-scrutiny empire like Thay. Here is the breakdown of why the Black Company (and by extension, the state) would attempt to claim your current inventory, even if you are acting on your 'own time':

**1. The Legal Fiction of 'Own Time':**
In the Black Company's bylaws, you are an active-duty Sergeant. There is no such thing as 'off-the-clock' when it comes to capturing state enemies or valuable slaves. Any assets seized by a squad are technically Company property until they are 'processed.' If you return to the Obsidian Ziggurat with five collared rebels, Captain Reynolds will expect the standard 30% management fee (or the Zulkirs will expect their tax) regardless of whether you were on a specific contract or a personal stroll.

**2. The Silt-River Precedent (How you avoid the claim):**
You have already demonstrated the solution to this problem: **The Audit.** 
- In Silt-River, you harvested 40 souls and reported them as 'burned in the fire.' Because there was no physical inventory to count, the Company couldn't claim a cut of those souls. 
- You only paid the Company their cut on the 10 slaves you actually 'booked' and the Elara asset. 

**3. The Risk of the Old Mill:**
Because you are currently at the Old Mill without a specific contract, any 'inventory' (the 5 rebels) you keep alive becomes a liability to your cover. If you bring them back 'on-book,' the Company takes their cut. If you keep them 'off-book,' you must hide them or liquidate them before returning to base. This is why the planning block suggests 'Liquidating the Sentries' via The Mint—it converts physical inventory the Company can claim into DPP that only you and Nocturne can see.

**4. Why they 'come' to claim it:**
They aren't physically following you right now, but the Thayan bureaucracy is built on surveillance. If you sell these rebels to a wizard in Bezantur or try to stash them in a Company-owned cell, the ledger-keepers will notice. In Thay, 'un-audited wealth' is treated as treason against the Zulkirs.

**Summary:** You have the freedom to do whatever you want on your own time, but to keep 100% of the profit, you must ensure the 'secular' world believes there was no profit to be had. Convert them to Souls or Soul Coins, and the Black Company has nothing to claim.

### **Administrative Audit: NPC Power Scaling & Level 22 Rationale**

You are correct that a **Level 22** NPC is an overwhelming threat to a **Level 6** character. In most standard D&D campaigns, this would be 'overkill.' However, in the *Sovereign’s Hunger* framework, this serves several specific mechanical and narrative purposes:

**1. The Tier System (Divine & Sovereign Tiers):**
This campaign uses an expanded level system defined in the `game_state_instruction.md` schema:
- **Mortal Tier (1-10):** Where you currently reside. Standard adventuring.
- **Divine Tier (21-25):** NPCs like Voresh Vane. These are entities who have touched the Abyssal link or high-circle Thayan magic. They are designed as 'Goalpost Antagonists.'
- **Sovereign Tier (26-30):** The upper echelon of the Zulkirs and Nocturne’s Honor Guard.
- **God-Tier (60+):** Nocturne Sosuke herself.

**2. Narrative Function (The 'Wall of the Maze'):**
Voresh and your older siblings are not designed to be fought *now*. They represent the walls of the maze you escaped. Their high levels quantify the 'century of silence' and the head start they have in the family's necromantic legacy. Seeing a Level 22 tag on an enemy tells the **Architect** (you) that this is a variable that cannot be solved with a direct assault yet—it requires infiltration, sabotage, and the **Soul Thief** engine to bridge the gap.

**3. Accelerated Growth (The Feast):**
Because you gain a **20% (or 100% on Feast) XP bonus** and consume the 'fragments' of elite victims, your progression is intended to be 3-5x faster than a normal NPC. You are a 'Singularity'—you will reach Level 20 while the rest of the world is still at Level 8. The Level 22 cap for Voresh ensures he remains a credible threat even as you skyrocket in power.

**4. CR vs. Level:**
In this system, we use 'Level' as a transparent shorthand for **Challenge Rating (CR)** to give you a clear tactical 'Audit' of your targets. A Level 22 NPC is roughly equivalent to a CR 20-21 creature (like an Ancient Dragon or a Lich).

**Would you like me to lower the 'Siblings' to the Mortal Tier (Levels 10-15) to make them more immediate targets, or keep them as 'Epic' goals for your ascension?**

### **Justification for the Scion Gap: The Battery vs. The Singularity**

The level discrepancy between you (Level 6) and your eldest siblings like Voresh (Level 22) is not just a numerical value; it is a fundamental part of the **House Vane** strategic geometry. Here is the narrative and mechanical justification for their 'Overwhelming' status:

**2. The Singularity Exception (Why you were cut out):**
Malakor's audit of your birth revealed that you were not a 'Battery' like the others; you were a **Singularity**. **3. The Evolution Curve (The Feast vs. Stagnation):**
Your siblings are powerful but **stagnant**. They have reached their ceiling within the Thayan system. You, however, possess **The Feast**. While it took Voresh thirty years to reach Level 22 through standard Thayan grinding, your ability to double XP and consume 'Fragments' means you can bridge that 16-level gap in a fraction of the time. You are designed to be a 'Low-Level' predator that out-paces 'High-Level' prey.

They represent the 'Wall of the Maze.'
